All 40 selected source–target pairs have now been examined

8 independently source-reviewed positive links · 23 additional worker-reviewed partial-match candidates · 9 reviewed relationships still unresolved. The 23 additions are not yet independently certified, and the nine open relationships are not rejected matches. The broader genealogy and author program remain open.

Inspect every named author and all 2,125 historical authorship links. Author links are not extra concepts, findings, or claims about individual contribution.

These eight selected pairings are source comparisons. Additional candidates, first-publication priority and complete program history require separate certification.

What these eight comparisons establish

SAN’s selected 2022 repository records contain source-backed partial connections with the selected later documents. This edition has 5 SAN framing families, 5 SAN documents, 8 located target passages, 3 exact target versions, 8 graded comparison links and 29 author-navigation edges. One link is an answer-to-question fit, not a shared asserted solution. Author edges identify paper membership, not individual conduct or extra wins.

Repository dates identify source records, not independently verified first-publication timestamps. Target dates identify exact selected versions, not first appearances of every idea.

Selected comparison space: 5 source operands × 3 versions × 8 document-qualified target atoms = 120 addresses. Of these, all 40 applicable cells have now received source-window review: 8 retain prior independent review, 23 additional partial-match candidates await independent review, and 9 reviewed relationships remain unresolved. Reviewing a cell is not the same as resolving its conceptual fit or certifying it. The other 80 address combinations refer to a target atom belonging to another document. The inherited full-program space remains 28,050 addresses / 2,805 applicable cells; this edition neither replaces nor closes it.

Search-tool and omission accounting

This release pass routed 14 SAN stages against five fixed target bodies: 70 bounded pairs. It used one TurboVec sidecar call, 14 Jigsaw/Theater calls and one census over 19 files. The census produced 540 operational atoms, 58 signature clusters, 201 partial-signature clusters, 230 fit patterns and 38 narrative similes. These are discovery outputs, not certified PECs. Census correspondence scoring was not run; zero correspondences cannot mean no match. JigsawRank reused an index; no fresh MemPalace retrieval is claimed.

The Wiki has no rival-award category. That policy is not proof that every historical decision followed it. Weak, overlapping, disputed and source-gated entries remain visible.

SAN proposed selective reception in its 2022 record; the 2025 Seth-coauthored review asks how receiving neurons use a message.

Card scope: 1 source framing; 1 SAN document; 1 target passage; 1 target document; 1 comparison link; 3 listed-author navigation edges.

Preserved finding: SETH-CS25-DOCWIN-SETH-CS25-22-SAN-AC012-MORPHOLOGY-SELECTIVE-PROJECTION-FE606E8CE4BC. Historical review annotation: challenge-not-admitted-preserved. Historical membership and rank remain unchanged.

SAN 2022-09-18, line 3 · Target 2025-10-30, PDF p. 9

Translated shared operation

Incoming message -> receiver-dependent use. Recognition by learned morphology and selective outgoing projection are the SAN proposal for the missing middle, not operations asserted by this target sentence.

Lens 1 · mechanism/function

SAN states a selective input-recognition-to-output operation. The target constrains the explanatory job at the receiver. This is a supported answer-to-question pairing, not implementation equivalence.

Lens 2 · ordered causal architecture

Both place the explanatory burden after signal arrival and before useful downstream processing. Only SAN joins learned receptive structure, recognition and projection in a proposed causal chain.

Lens 3 · work backward from the constrained result

Constrained result: A broadcast carries information that downstream neural processing can use. Required operation: There must be a physically implemented dependence of receiver processing on relevant message differences; arrival alone does not explain use. Source test: Line 3 supplies learned selectivity followed by transmission; lines 49–61 elaborate input-sensitive response learning. Alternatives: Learned receptive selectivity; Fixed feature-sensitive circuitry; Population decoding or predictive message passing. Remaining uncertainty: The question neither selects this mechanism nor proves the claimed receiver operation explains consciousness.

Chronology, earlier-source boundaries and remaining differences

The verified SAN Git record is earlier than this selected target version. First-publication timing and firstness across the target’s earlier history are not certified by that date ordering.

The selected passage questions global-broadcast explanations. This card compares the explanatory job with SAN’s proposed receiver mechanism; it does not claim the question adopts that mechanism.

The apical-dendrite/exit-terminal conflation in the same SAN neighborhood is not endorsed. Quoted layer-5/layer-6 studies are prior sources, not Micah's experiments. Full neural semantics and experiential sufficiency remain open.

Formal relation and target attribution

Partial role/subchain translation only. No object-and-morphism construction or proof of functoriality is supplied. The common input/transform/output dependencies are stated explicitly; no transition-system bisimulation or process-calculus equivalence is proved.

The authors request a mechanistic explanation of a global broadcast's reception; they do not endorse an implementation.

Citation coverage: not evaluated for omission in this edition.

SAN’s 2022 record proposes measured-pattern BCI reconstruction before the selected 2025 intervention agenda.

Card scope: 1 source framing; 1 SAN document; 1 target passage; 1 target document; 1 comparison link; 3 listed-author navigation edges.

Preserved finding: SETH-CS25-DOCWIN-SETH-CS25-38-SAN-AC013-QUALIA-RECONSTRUCTION-4377D46F92F0. Historical review annotation: calibrated-preserved. Historical membership and rank remain unchanged.

SAN 2022-06-12, line 4 · Target 2025-10-30, PDF p. 14

Translated shared operation

Identify an experience-relevant neural organization -> intervene on that organization -> intentionally change experience.

Lens 1 · mechanism/function

Both propose a causal intervention on neural mechanisms to affect experience. SAN specifies reproducing a learned distributed pattern; the target specifies neither that code nor faithful replay of a particular sensory episode.

Lens 2 · ordered causal architecture

Neural account -> intervention target -> altered experience is common. SAN adds measurement of personal sensory patterns and proposed thalamic stimulation; target purposes include remediation and enhancement rather than only recreation.

Lens 3 · work backward from the constrained result

Constrained result: A circuit intervention intentionally remediates or enhances a conscious experience. Required operation: The manipulated circuit variable must causally affect the experiential feature, with sufficient targeting to separate it from a merely behavioral effect. Source test: Lines 4 and 16 propose measuring a sensory pattern and stimulating the thalamus with a corresponding pattern to trigger experience. Alternatives: Electrical activity-pattern stimulation; Neurochemical modulation; Other content-relevant neural codes. Remaining uncertainty: The target does not require SAN's code, anatomical location, replay fidelity or proposed BCI. Neither text demonstrates this SAN procedure.

Chronology, earlier-source boundaries and remaining differences

The verified SAN Git record is earlier than this selected target version. First-publication timing and firstness across the target’s earlier history are not certified by that date ordering.

SAN’s source names sensory-substitution examples, David Eagleman, Jeff Hawkins and Tim Mullen. Their component work remains visible; the compared SAN contribution is the proposed measured-pattern reconstruction chain.

SAN explicitly names sensory-substitution examples, David Eagleman, Hawkins and Tim Mullen. Those components remain prior-work boundaries; the proposed synthesis is not evidence of inventing them.

Formal relation and target attribution

Partial role/subchain translation only. No object-and-morphism construction or proof of functoriality is supplied. The common input/transform/output dependencies are stated explicitly; no transition-system bisimulation or process-calculus equivalence is proved.

Conditional future intervention agenda: better biological understanding should enable circuit-level interventions, including BCIs, that affect experience.

Citation coverage: not evaluated for omission in this edition.

Preserved finding: SETH-CS25-DOCWIN-SETH-CS25-30-SAN-AC013-QUALIA-RECONSTRUCTION-1551F6988344. Historical review annotation: challenge-not-admitted-preserved. Historical membership and rank remain unchanged.

SAN 2022-06-12, line 4 · Target 2025-10-30, PDF p. 12

Translated shared operation

Relate neural states to conscious experience -> identify a causally relevant target -> use it for deliberate experiential control.

Lens 1 · mechanism/function

The target's prospective ability to control experience contains the goal served by SAN's proposed sensory-pattern recreation. SAN does not supply all conscious-state identification or arbitrary precise control.

Lens 2 · ordered causal architecture

Understanding the neural basis is meant to make intervention possible in both. The target includes distinguishing conscious from nonconscious states, which SAN's broccoli example does not establish.

Lens 3 · work backward from the constrained result

Constrained result: A tool controls consciousness as precisely as tools aim to control behavior. Required operation: Identify manipulable neural variables with reliable experiential consequences; behavioral control alone is insufficient. Source test: SAN line 4 proposes a causal replay mechanism and line 16 adds individual-pattern measurement and direct stimulation. Alternatives: Pattern-specific stimulation; State-level neuromodulation; Other experimentally calibrated neural interventions. Remaining uncertainty: Recreating one proposed content does not derive a general test for consciousness or the target's precision of control.

Chronology, earlier-source boundaries and remaining differences

The verified SAN Git record is earlier than this selected target version. First-publication timing and firstness across the target’s earlier history are not certified by that date ordering.

The review cites Thomas Metzinger’s The Ego Tunnel (2009), reference 22 and footnote 3, for phenotechnology. The exact earlier mechanism is not adjudicated here; this is a later-document partial comparison, not firstness over that lineage.

This is a second passage in the same target document and reuses the BCI row's SAN concept. Reference 22 / footnote 3 identifies Metzinger's 2009 phenotechnology antecedent; no firstness over that line is established.

Formal relation and target attribution

Partial role/subchain translation only. No object-and-morphism construction or proof of functoriality is supplied. The common input/transform/output dependencies are stated explicitly; no transition-system bisimulation or process-calculus equivalence is proved.

A forward-looking consequence of success in consciousness science, with phenotechnology explicitly cited to Metzinger.

SAN’s 2022 geometry proposal connects learned neural patterns to experiential properties before the selected 2025 review.

Card scope: 1 source framing; 1 SAN document; 1 target passage; 1 target document; 1 comparison link; 3 listed-author navigation edges.

Preserved finding: SETH-CS25-DOCWIN-SETH-CS25-28-SAN-AC009-PHASE-TOPOLOGY-TAYLOR-6DCF83E437C1. Historical review annotation: supported. Historical membership and rank remain unchanged.

SAN 2022-07-09, line 18 · Target 2025-10-30, PDF p. 12

Translated shared operation

Structured neural activity -> representation of particular experiential shapes or properties. The target's additional pairwise similarity relations are not established in SAN.

Lens 1 · mechanism/function

SAN ties learned spatial-temporal phase patterns to shapes and properties of experience; the target ties neural state-space organization to visual experience. This is more specific than the word 'geometry', but not the same metric or data analysis.

Lens 2 · ordered causal architecture

Neural organization is used to explain experiential content in both. SAN's constructive learning account does not itself derive relational similarity across experiences or subjects, the target's distinctive explanatory step.

Lens 3 · work backward from the constrained result

Constrained result: Neural similarities systematically correspond to similarities among experiences. Required operation: A representation and comparison rule must retain the distinctions and relations used in perceptual judgments. Source test: Lines 16–18 provide structured learned patterns and experiential properties; line 39 additionally calls phase topology a brain-state attractor. No pairwise similarity rule is supplied. Alternatives: Population-rate geometry; Learned temporal-pattern geometry; Other representational spaces with a separately tested neural-to-perceptual map. Remaining uncertainty: The attractor remark strengthens a state/experience bridge, not evidence for a metric, similarity judgment model or empirical validation.

Chronology, earlier-source boundaries and remaining differences

The verified SAN Git record is earlier than this selected target version. First-publication timing and firstness across the target’s earlier history are not certified by that date ordering.

The review cites Chang and Tsao (2017), Malach (2021), A. Clark (2000), Rosenthal (2010), Tsuchiya and Saigo (2021), and later quality-space studies. These are named source boundaries, not findings that their complete operations equal SAN. The pairwise similarity metric remains outside this selected SAN excerpt.

A within-experience contour or shape is not automatically a relation among different experiences. Target refs 45–48 and 200–206 include pre-2022 work; their cited presence blocks an unqualified 2022-versus-2025 origin story but is not a completed predecessor adjudication.

Formal relation and target attribution

Partial role/subchain translation only. No object-and-morphism construction or proof of functoriality is supplied. The common input/transform/output dependencies are stated explicitly; no transition-system bisimulation or process-calculus equivalence is proved.

Synthesis of empirical neural/perceptual similarity studies and a philosophical quality-space hypothesis, followed by an open integration question.

SAN’s 2022 record documents world-responsive predictive rendering, with a partial match to the selected 2025 review.

Card scope: 1 source framing; 1 SAN document; 1 target passage; 1 target document; 1 comparison link; 3 listed-author navigation edges.

Preserved finding: SETH-CS25-DOCWIN-SETH-CS25-18-SAN-AC017-DISTRIBUTED-SELF-RENDERING-B75285FE18A0. Historical review annotation: supported. Historical membership and rank remain unchanged.

SAN 2022-06-10, line 19 · Target 2025-10-30, PDF p. 6

Translated shared operation

World-sensitive information -> internally maintained predictive representation -> experienced content.

Lens 1 · mechanism/function

SAN explicitly combines predictive modeling, world reactivity and internally read patterns. The target makes the sharper claim that top-down predictions provide content and sensory signals constrain them.

Lens 2 · ordered causal architecture

Internal modeling coupled to environmental information is shared. SAN's sparse excitation/inhibition departures and downstream receivers are not the target's stated Bayesian prediction-error algorithm.

Lens 3 · work backward from the constrained result

Constrained result: An internally generated percept is controlled by the world rather than unconstrained hallucination. Required operation: An external-sensitive route must constrain or update the represented content. Source test: Line 19 explicitly supplies world-reactive predictive patterns, and line 21 links sensory interactions to successive receiving arrays. Alternatives: Prediction-error minimization; Other recurrent estimators; World-sensitive selection or correction of internal patterns. Remaining uncertainty: World reactivity does not uniquely imply Bayesian error minimization or a particular top-down hierarchy; no conscious/unconscious discriminator follows.

Chronology, earlier-source boundaries and remaining differences

The verified SAN Git record is earlier than this selected target version. First-publication timing and firstness across the target’s earlier history are not certified by that date ordering.

The 2025 passage cites Anil Seth’s Being You (2021), reference 23. The 2022-versus-2025 dates identify these selected records, not the first appearance of Seth’s predictive-processing account. Earlier SAN stages and the full before/after comparison remain open.

The exact 2025 occurrence is later than the Git record, but its reference 23 is Seth, Being You (2021). The target also states that PP does not by itself specify necessary/sufficient consciousness conditions. Do not imply SAN preceded Seth's predictive-processing work.

Formal relation and target attribution

Partial role/subchain translation only. No object-and-morphism construction or proof of functoriality is supplied. The common input/transform/output dependencies are stated explicitly; no transition-system bisimulation or process-calculus equivalence is proved.

Review of predictive-processing theories and the controlled-hallucination formulation, expressly citing Seth's 2021 book.

SAN’s 2022 predictive-rendering record supplies an earlier model-to-experience connection than the selected May 2026 Rosetta version.

Card scope: 1 source framing; 1 SAN document; 1 target passage; 1 target document; 1 comparison link; 5 listed-author navigation edges.

Preserved finding: SETH-SANFULL-086. Historical review annotation: calibrated-preserved. Historical membership and rank remain unchanged.

SAN 2022-06-10, line 19 · Target 2026-05-04, PDF p. 5

Translated shared operation

World/sensory information -> an internal model state -> a proposed relation to experiential content.

Lens 1 · mechanism/function

SAN's internally rendered predictive model and the target's belief-to-phenomenology mapping occupy a related explanatory role. SAN does not define approximate posterior distributions or the map phi.

Lens 2 · ordered causal architecture

Both use an internal representation between neural dynamics and experience. The target makes beliefs a common formal currency linking multiple observables, whereas SAN proposes a physical receiver/dynamics realization.

Lens 3 · work backward from the constrained result

Constrained result: Phenomenological content is determined by the modeled belief state. Required operation: Under that assumption, the belief state and mapping must retain distinctions sufficient to determine experiential content; identical modeled beliefs cannot map to different content without added variables. Source test: SAN lines 17–21 connect temporally distributed representations with consciousness and receiving arrays; they do not establish a probability space or sufficiency of beliefs alone. Alternatives: Identity mapping; Marginalization onto a subset; Pushforward/coarse-graining; Other deterministic, potentially many-to-one mapping. Remaining uncertainty: The reverse argument tests compatibility with a stated assumption. It does not prove the assumption or uniquely identify SAN as its implementation.

Chronology, earlier-source boundaries and remaining differences

The verified SAN Git record is earlier than this selected target version. First-publication timing and firstness across the target’s earlier history are not certified by that date ordering.

The selected v3 body names Varela’s neurophenomenology, predictive coding, Bayesian-brain accounts and 2021/2022 generative-passage work. Those cited lineages remain open controls. The May 2026 wording is not backdated to the inherited v1 key.

The body is arXiv v3, 4 May 2026, not its inherited v1 key/date. The target's pp. 2–4 name Varela, predictive coding, Bayesian-brain and 2021/2022 generative-passage predecessors; none is erased or awarded a new priority verdict here.

Formal relation and target attribution

Partial role/subchain translation only. No object-and-morphism construction or proof of functoriality is supplied. The common input/transform/output dependencies are stated explicitly; no transition-system bisimulation or process-calculus equivalence is proved.

An explicitly conditional technical assumption, followed by predictions; not a proved fact or a full theory deciding where consciousness exists.

SAN’s 2022 geometry record supplies an earlier representation-to-experience connection than the selected 2026 Rosetta formulation.

Card scope: 1 source framing; 1 SAN document; 1 target passage; 1 target document; 1 comparison link; 5 listed-author navigation edges.

Preserved finding: SETH-SANFULL-033. Historical review annotation: calibrated-preserved. Historical membership and rank remain unchanged.

SAN 2022-07-09, line 18 · Target 2026-05-04, PDF p. 4

Translated shared operation

Structured internal representation -> characterization of experiential properties. A shared pairwise/inter-subject distance rule is not established.

Lens 1 · mechanism/function

SAN gives a physically learned pattern-to-experiential-shape proposal. The target characterizes differences in probability-distribution content and conditionally induces phenomenological geometry. That role-level subset survives, not the metric.

Lens 2 · ordered causal architecture

The common prefix is representational structure informing experience. Worker L2's 'relations among experiences' goes beyond SAN's shown intra-experience shapes/properties. The target adds an explicit belief-to-phenomenology map and information-geometric distances.

Lens 3 · work backward from the constrained result

Constrained result: A geometry can characterize experiential differences across subjects or moments. Required operation: Specify a state space, a discrepancy/metric, and a justified relation between that discrepancy and experiential difference. Source test: SAN lines 6, 16–18 and 39 describe changing structured neural patterns and experiential properties, but supply neither a probability-distribution metric nor an inter-subject comparison map. Alternatives: Fisher information distance under the target's conditions; Other divergences or metrics explicitly allowed by the target; A neural-pattern geometry with an independently supplied experiential comparison map. Remaining uncertainty: Different neural patterns can support different experiential properties without fixing their distances. SAN does not derive Fisher geometry, confidence effects or the paper's predictions.

Chronology, earlier-source boundaries and remaining differences

The verified SAN Git record is earlier than this selected target version. First-publication timing and firstness across the target’s earlier history are not certified by that date ordering.

The v3 paper’s related-work section preserves Varela and predictive-processing/generative-passage antecedents. SAN’s Taylor-series and attractor statements are proposals, not a proof of this paper’s Fisher metric or predictions.

Taylor-series/tensor language is offered as a thought experiment and analogy, not a proved approximation theorem or a demonstrated metric. Keeping this limit does not erase the explicit neural-pattern/experience bridge.

Formal relation and target attribution

Partial role/subchain translation only. No object-and-morphism construction or proof of functoriality is supplied. The common input/transform/output dependencies are stated explicitly; no transition-system bisimulation or process-calculus equivalence is proved.

The authors derive a conditional information-geometric framework; their Fisher metric is a natural choice, not uniquely mandated.

SAN’s 2022 artificial-neurology record shares recurrent computation with the selected 2024 reservoir study.

Card scope: 1 source framing; 1 SAN document; 1 target passage; 1 target document; 1 comparison link; 4 listed-author navigation edges.

Preserved finding: SETH-SANFULL-122. Historical review annotation: supported. Historical membership and rank remain unchanged.

SAN 2022-11-28, line 19 · Target 2024-06-27, PDF p. 1

Translated shared operation

Incoming activity -> stateful recurrent nonlinear processing -> output available for subsequent computation.

Lens 1 · mechanism/function

Both descriptions use recurrent neural dynamics over time with downstream outputs. SAN additionally proposes changing connections and experience; the target fixes reservoir weights and trains a linear readout.

Lens 2 · ordered causal architecture

The shared input/state/output dependency is real and survives implementation differences. A loop alone does not establish reservoir training, a task loss, or the target's bidirectional performance/emergence result.

Lens 3 · work backward from the constrained result

Constrained result: Forecast temporal trajectories from the observed input history; the selected paper realizes this goal with a reservoir and learned readout. Required operation: Encode task-relevant input/history in usable state distinctions and map those distinctions to predictions. Source test: SAN line 19 provides time-extended signaling loops, nonlinear spatial-temporal responses and subsequent arrays. It does not specify a fixed reservoir or readout-only optimization. Alternatives: Reservoir recurrence plus trained readout; Other recurrent state models; History windows or delay-line features in nonrecurrent predictors. Remaining uncertainty: SAN's loop is a plausible architectural ingredient, not a derivation of the target equations, optimization or PID emergence measure.

Chronology, earlier-source boundaries and remaining differences

The verified SAN Git record is earlier than this selected target version. First-publication timing and firstness across the target’s earlier history are not certified by that date ordering.

The paper cites Jaeger (2001), Maass and colleagues (2002), and Buonomano and Maass (2009) for reservoir/state-dependent computing; it cites Rosas/Mediano and colleagues (2020) for emergence. This is a preserved partial comparison with the 2024 document, not a claim that SAN originated reservoir computing.

The target cites Jaeger (2001), Maass et al. (2002), Buonomano/Maass (2009) for the architecture and Rosas/Mediano et al. (2020) for emergence. These citations are prior-work boundaries, not a new author-wide award.

Formal relation and target attribution

Partial role/subchain translation only. No object-and-morphism construction or proof of functoriality is supplied. The common input/transform/output dependencies are stated explicitly; no transition-system bisimulation or process-calculus equivalence is proved.

Description of an established reservoir-computing architecture used in the authors' prediction/emergence study; not a claim to invent recurrence.

SETH-SELECTED-40-01 · Previously independently source-reviewed subset

SAN-AC012-MORPHOLOGY-SELECTIVE-PROJECTION → SETH-CS25-22

SAN describes a receiver recognizing a learned pattern and passing selected activity onward. The later paper asks for an explanation of that step; it does not assert SAN’s answer.

SAN: 2022-09-18 · Git repository commit record. Target: 2025-10-30 · doi:10.3389/fsci.2025.1546279.

Current anonymous public access to the exact source body was verified on 2026-10-11. The displayed 2022 date is a Git record date, not independently verified first-publication timing.

Target authors: Axel Cleeremans · Liad Mudrik · Anil K. Seth. Verify the target passage · See its short quotation in the paired cards.

Exact SAN source windows

SAN lines 3–3

Dendritic morphologies & synaptic connections represent learned memories. Memories activate when preferred patterns (the memory) are detected, this causes cells to create a selective transmission or a projection to other cells (neural arrays). In this note I am looking at Layer 6 & Layer 5 Pyramidal cells, to learn how the Apical Dendrite, or the Exit Terminal, is contributing to the rendered projection of that learned memory to the rest of its network. The main idea that I am exploring is that the signal transmitted from the exit terminal is not uniform along all branches, but instead is modified synapses by synapse, and that the memories in the exit terminal also transmit backwards to play a role in multiple seconds long coincidence detections involving back propagating action potentials from the exit terminal or Apical Dendrite to the Soma, that when combined with the Basal or sensory input dendrite can cause a coincidence detection that creates a special type of action potential event called burst firing.

L1

result: PARTIAL_FUNCTIONAL_PROBLEM_FIT

reason: SAN states a selective input-recognition-to-output operation. The target constrains the explanatory job at the receiver. This is a supported answer-to-question pairing, not implementation equivalence.

L2

result: PARTIAL_ARCHITECTURAL_LOCATION

reason: Both place the explanatory burden after signal arrival and before useful downstream processing. Only SAN joins learned receptive structure, recognition and projection in a proposed causal chain.

L3

result: SUPPORTED_CONSTRAINED_OUTPUT_RECONSTRUCTION_WITH_NONUNIQUE_SOLUTION

reason: Constrained result: A broadcast carries information that downstream neural processing can use. Required operation: There must be a physically implemented dependence of receiver processing on relevant message differences; arrival alone does not explain use. Source test: Line 3 supplies learned selectivity followed by transmission; lines 49–61 elaborate input-sensitive response learning. Alternatives: Learned receptive selectivity; Fixed feature-sensitive circuitry; Population decoding or predictive message passing. Remaining uncertainty: The question neither selects this mechanism nor proves the claimed receiver operation explains consciousness.

Limits and next evidence

The apical-dendrite/exit-terminal conflation in the same SAN neighborhood is not endorsed. Quoted layer-5/layer-6 studies are prior sources, not Micah's experiments. Full neural semantics and experiential sufficiency remain open.

At most a qualitative functional, conceptual or causal-prefix relation; no proof of functoriality, isomorphism, bisimulation, exact equation identity or calibrated statistical dependence.

Existing historical IDs for this exact source-atom/target-atom pair: SETH-CS25-DOCWIN-SETH-CS25-22-SAN-AC012-MORPHOLOGY-SELECTIVE-PROJECTION-FE606E8CE4BC.

SETH-SELECTED-40-02 · Source reviewed; relationship unresolved

SAN-AC012-MORPHOLOGY-SELECTIVE-PROJECTION → SETH-CS25-38

A selective receiver could be an intervention substrate, but the selected SAN operation is recognition and projection, not a stated BCI intervention.

SAN: 2022-09-18 · Git repository commit record. Target: 2025-10-30 · doi:10.3389/fsci.2025.1546279.

Current anonymous public access to the exact source body was verified on 2026-10-11. The displayed 2022 date is a Git record date, not independently verified first-publication timing.

Target authors: Axel Cleeremans · Liad Mudrik · Anil K. Seth. Verify the target passage · See its short quotation in the paired cards.

Exact SAN source windows

SAN lines 3–3

Dendritic morphologies & synaptic connections represent learned memories. Memories activate when preferred patterns (the memory) are detected, this causes cells to create a selective transmission or a projection to other cells (neural arrays). In this note I am looking at Layer 6 & Layer 5 Pyramidal cells, to learn how the Apical Dendrite, or the Exit Terminal, is contributing to the rendered projection of that learned memory to the rest of its network. The main idea that I am exploring is that the signal transmitted from the exit terminal is not uniform along all branches, but instead is modified synapses by synapse, and that the memories in the exit terminal also transmit backwards to play a role in multiple seconds long coincidence detections involving back propagating action potentials from the exit terminal or Apical Dendrite to the Soma, that when combined with the Basal or sensory input dendrite can cause a coincidence detection that creates a special type of action potential event called burst firing.

SAN lines 49–61

I think of it as like the basal dendrite is learning to respond selectively to certain patterns, the apical is learning what patterns it responds to. My intuition is that the apical is learning its own cell's response, and through feedback loops, with itself and with other pyramidal neurons the cell is going to make predictions about its own patterns of causation, what patterns it responds & how it reacts or responds. With the Apical Dendrite perhaps symbolizing a more complex response that is connected to more complex learning. (compared to other types of neurons) The fact that bursting (bursts of somatic AP) results from the coincident activity of basal + apical, but not from either one separately is a different coincidence detection mechanism from the leaky integrate-and fire model of a neuron which explores coincident synaptic inputs, leading to coincident dendritic activity, which leads to the phasic action potential from just the south side of the Soma. In the leaky integrate & fire neurons, and the Hodgkin & Huxley Model there is very little thought to the concept of the Soma burst spiking being a coincidence detection from both sides of the neuron. But if it's true for Layer 5 pyramidal cells, then we ought to know if there is a greater role for the exit terminal in all other types of neurons. So perhaps we can think of the axon, with it's different types of action potentials, different durations (wave shapes, magnitudes), and different burst rates, as a analogous to a lens, a lense that is creating a summation signal that the exit terminal is learning as it's learned response. The idea is that our sensory inputs are sort of encoded in the input dendrite (or the basal dendrite) and our motor outputs are sort of encoded in the exit terminal (the Apical dendrite), and through cyclic looping activity our input learning (sensory in the bottom dendrite) is being mapped to our output learning (motor in the output exit terminal, or the Apical Dendrite) So every neuron represents an actor, one that learns to respond selectively to certain patterns, and encodes in its exit a response action.

L1

result: OPEN-NO-CELL-CLOSURE

reason: A selective receiver could be an intervention substrate, but the selected SAN operation is recognition and projection, not a stated BCI intervention.

L2

result: OPEN-CAUSAL-BRIDGE

source chain: Learned receptive structure -> preferred-pattern recognition -> selective neural projection; feedback supports predictions of the cell's own response.

target chain: Understanding biological basis -> circuit-level intervention -> changed conscious experience.

reason: A selective receiver could be an intervention substrate, but the selected SAN operation is recognition and projection, not a stated BCI intervention.

residual: Recover an explicit experience-reconstruction or interface bridge for this source-stage family; do not infer controllability of experience from selectivity alone.

L3

result: BACKWARD-TEST-COMPLETED-BRIDGE-OPEN

constrained output: A brain-computer intervention targets, remediates or enhances an aspect of conscious experience.

required operation: An intervention must alter a biological state in a way linked to an experiential consequence.

source test: Changing a learned input pattern might change receiver output. This supplies a possible component, not a demonstrated translation of the target's experience-directed intervention.

alternative routes: Electrical stimulation; Sensory substitution or feedback interfaces; Pharmacological or optogenetic circuit modulation

remaining uncertainty: Recover an explicit experience-reconstruction or interface bridge for this source-stage family; do not infer controllability of experience from selectivity alone.

Limits and next evidence

Recover an explicit experience-reconstruction or interface bridge for this source-stage family; do not infer controllability of experience from selectivity alone.

At most a qualitative functional, conceptual or causal-prefix relation; no proof of functoriality, isomorphism, bisimulation, exact equation identity or calibrated statistical dependence.

SETH-SELECTED-40-06 · Source reviewed; relationship unresolved

SAN-AC012-MORPHOLOGY-SELECTIVE-PROJECTION → SETH-N24-01

Learned memory representations and belief states are related candidate notions, but the selected receptor/projection account is not yet a source-bound belief-to-phenomenology map.

SAN: 2022-09-18 · Git repository commit record. Target: 2026-05-04 · arxiv:2409.20318v3.

Current anonymous public access to the exact source body was verified on 2026-10-11. The displayed 2022 date is a Git record date, not independently verified first-publication timing.

Exact SAN source windows

SAN lines 3–3

Dendritic morphologies & synaptic connections represent learned memories. Memories activate when preferred patterns (the memory) are detected, this causes cells to create a selective transmission or a projection to other cells (neural arrays). In this note I am looking at Layer 6 & Layer 5 Pyramidal cells, to learn how the Apical Dendrite, or the Exit Terminal, is contributing to the rendered projection of that learned memory to the rest of its network. The main idea that I am exploring is that the signal transmitted from the exit terminal is not uniform along all branches, but instead is modified synapses by synapse, and that the memories in the exit terminal also transmit backwards to play a role in multiple seconds long coincidence detections involving back propagating action potentials from the exit terminal or Apical Dendrite to the Soma, that when combined with the Basal or sensory input dendrite can cause a coincidence detection that creates a special type of action potential event called burst firing.

SAN lines 49–61

I think of it as like the basal dendrite is learning to respond selectively to certain patterns, the apical is learning what patterns it responds to. My intuition is that the apical is learning its own cell's response, and through feedback loops, with itself and with other pyramidal neurons the cell is going to make predictions about its own patterns of causation, what patterns it responds & how it reacts or responds. With the Apical Dendrite perhaps symbolizing a more complex response that is connected to more complex learning. (compared to other types of neurons) The fact that bursting (bursts of somatic AP) results from the coincident activity of basal + apical, but not from either one separately is a different coincidence detection mechanism from the leaky integrate-and fire model of a neuron which explores coincident synaptic inputs, leading to coincident dendritic activity, which leads to the phasic action potential from just the south side of the Soma. In the leaky integrate & fire neurons, and the Hodgkin & Huxley Model there is very little thought to the concept of the Soma burst spiking being a coincidence detection from both sides of the neuron. But if it's true for Layer 5 pyramidal cells, then we ought to know if there is a greater role for the exit terminal in all other types of neurons. So perhaps we can think of the axon, with it's different types of action potentials, different durations (wave shapes, magnitudes), and different burst rates, as a analogous to a lens, a lense that is creating a summation signal that the exit terminal is learning as it's learned response. The idea is that our sensory inputs are sort of encoded in the input dendrite (or the basal dendrite) and our motor outputs are sort of encoded in the exit terminal (the Apical dendrite), and through cyclic looping activity our input learning (sensory in the bottom dendrite) is being mapped to our output learning (motor in the output exit terminal, or the Apical Dendrite) So every neuron represents an actor, one that learns to respond selectively to certain patterns, and encodes in its exit a response action.

L1

result: OPEN-NO-CELL-CLOSURE

reason: Learned memory representations and belief states are related candidate notions, but the selected receptor/projection account is not yet a source-bound belief-to-phenomenology map.

L2

result: OPEN-CAUSAL-BRIDGE

source chain: Learned receptive structure -> preferred-pattern recognition -> selective neural projection; feedback supports predictions of the cell's own response.

target chain: Internal state encodes approximate posterior -> belief-to-phenomenology function -> experiential content.

reason: Learned memory representations and belief states are related candidate notions, but the selected receptor/projection account is not yet a source-bound belief-to-phenomenology map.

residual: Recover the experience and belief-role bridge. Do not equate memory, all neural output and phenomenology merely by renaming them.

L3

result: BACKWARD-TEST-COMPLETED-BRIDGE-OPEN

constrained output: Phenomenological content is represented as a function of beliefs.

required operation: The selected hypothesis links a specified internal belief state to phenomenological content; the paper does not claim every neural representation is a posterior belief.

source test: The target assumes a function from posterior beliefs to experience. SAN supplies pattern-selective neural output; a translation from that output to both operands of the target function remains incomplete.

alternative routes: Identity map; Marginalization; A coarser non-injective mapping

remaining uncertainty: Recover the experience and belief-role bridge. Do not equate memory, all neural output and phenomenology merely by renaming them.

Limits and next evidence

Recover the experience and belief-role bridge. Do not equate memory, all neural output and phenomenology merely by renaming them.

SAN-AC013-QUALIA-RECONSTRUCTION → SETH-CS25-22

SAN proposes temporal alignment between oscillating regions so a receiving network can detect correlations in another region's signals.

SAN: 2022-06-12 · Git repository commit record. Target: 2025-10-30 · doi:10.3389/fsci.2025.1546279.

Current anonymous public access to the exact source body was verified on 2026-10-11. The displayed 2022 date is a Git record date, not independently verified first-publication timing.

Exact SAN source windows

SAN lines 2–2

Brocoli Properties example: All the properties of Brocoli are features learned by the neural network of the human brain. So the color, texture, feel, crunch, taste, smell these are learned patterns, information patterns are detected as coincidence evens by receptors on dendrites, then by neurons, then by arrays of neurons, then by nuclei in the thalamus, then by cortical columns & the hippocampal entorhinal circuit, then by the thalamus again, essentially circuiting throughout your brain in a spatial temporal oscillating feedback loop.

SAN lines 12–16

The main idea that I have about the importance of brainwave synchrony between brain regions when objects become conscious comes from thinking about Tim Mullen's neuropype, and how he aligned unstructure data like EEG, EMG, ECG (Brain, Muscle, Heart sensors) by understanding the assumptions made in each technology and figuring out how to correctly align the signal differences temporally, so that when a brainwave spikes, one might associate it with a heart rate spike, given a time based association. In the same way if one part of the brain wants to understand unstructured data from another part of the brain, I suspect synchrony (achieved by listening to one's self) is creating a temporal alignment between two oscillators so that correlations between the two sets of unstructured data can be found by the neural network of your mind, and you make sense out of an association between lightning & thunder for example. So if we knew how the properties of brocoli were represented by scanning them at the Thalamus at the moment you were thinking about them, then we might identify which cortical column is managing those properties when you are not thinking about them, and even if you have a completely different personality that manages brocoli in a different region of the cortex, by stimulating the thalamus with the properties of brocoli that we learned from watching your thalamus we might trigger the experience of brocoli in your brain, we might have a next generation brain computer interface for bringing Augmented Reality & Virtual Reality experiences not through glasses but through direct brain stimulation, to recreate known patterns.

L1

result: PARTIAL-PROBLEM-FIT

reason: SAN proposes temporal alignment between oscillating regions so a receiving network can detect correlations in another region's signals.

L2

result: PARTIAL-PROBLEM-FIT

source chain: Learned multisensory patterns circulate through a neural feedback loop -> measured phase-pattern configuration -> proposed pattern reinstatement to recreate an experience.

target chain: Broadcast message -> unspecified receiver interpretation -> useful downstream processing.

reason: SAN proposes temporal alignment between oscillating regions so a receiving network can detect correlations in another region's signals.

residual: The later review asks a question; it does not adopt the synchrony proposal or experimentally validate it.

L3

result: BOUNDED-BACKWARD-TEST

constrained output: A receiving neuron makes useful use of a broadcast message.

required operation: Some implemented receiver dependency must discriminate consequential input differences; arrival alone does not explain use.

source test: To use a received message, a network needs a response-sensitive relation to signal differences. SAN line 14 supplies a concrete alignment-and-correlation proposal for that explanatory job.

alternative routes: Learned receptive selectivity; Fixed feature-sensitive circuitry; Population-level decoding

remaining uncertainty: The later review asks a question; it does not adopt the synchrony proposal or experimentally validate it.

SAN-AC013-QUALIA-RECONSTRUCTION → SETH-CS25-38

Both link an explanation of experience to an intervention that changes it. SAN proposes measuring a person’s sensory pattern and using a corresponding pattern to recreate experience.

SAN: 2022-06-12 · Git repository commit record. Target: 2025-10-30 · doi:10.3389/fsci.2025.1546279.

Current anonymous public access to the exact source body was verified on 2026-10-11. The displayed 2022 date is a Git record date, not independently verified first-publication timing.

The point of the Brocoli Properties example was to show that if we could intercept at the Thalamus how the properties of Brocoli are signals being passed to the neo cortex, then we could recreate the experience of Brocoli in the Thalamus by creating that set of temporal & spatially distributed phase changes. This could be done at any point in the brain, or on any part of the body, as illustrated in the example of the tongue strip with a grid of electrical signals that correspond to the pixel changes of a camera's viewfinder, a device that allows people to see through their tongue or via any part of their body.

result: SUPPORTED_CONSTRAINED_OUTPUT_RECONSTRUCTION_WITH_NONUNIQUE_SOLUTION

Required operation: The manipulated circuit variable must causally affect the experiential feature, with sufficient targeting to separate it from a merely behavioral effect. Source test: Lines 4 and 16 propose measuring a sensory pattern and stimulating the thalamus with a corresponding pattern to trigger experience. Alternatives: Electrical activity-pattern stimulation; Neurochemical modulation; Other content-relevant neural codes. Remaining uncertainty: The target does not require SAN's code, anatomical location, replay fidelity or proposed BCI. Neither text demonstrates this SAN procedure.

There are papers that suggest the hippocampus is like a cortical column, and or a comparator to cortical column data, maybe its cortical column #1, and maybe that is where the base rendering of consciousness begins, or likes to sit, like the central locus of focus, but the information in just the hippocampal-enthorhinal loop might be like you, the room, the walls, and where you are in the world, and following the book A Thousand Brains by Jeff Hawkins it might be true that each of your other cortical columns gets busy representing other objects, sounds, concepts, and things that are part of your experience in the room, so the cup on the table, and the hot sauce, are each managed by different cortical columns, all the properties of the cup might be sent to a cortical column that is managing the sensory integration for that object, its orientation, position, temperature, texture, sound etc... and when your awareness of that cup increase that is plausibly going to be an increase in the syncronization between that cortical column and the hippocampal entorhinal loop & the parietal temperal junction all at once, this is something we could look for with current medical imaging technology, whether there is brainwave frequency synchronization between hippocampal frequencies and the parietal temporal junction and or specific cortical columns. The main idea that I have about the importance of brainwave synchrony between brain regions when objects become conscious comes from thinking about Tim Mullen's neuropype, and how he aligned unstructure data like EEG, EMG, ECG (Brain, Muscle, Heart sensors) by understanding the assumptions made in each technology and figuring out how to correctly align the signal differences temporally, so that when a brainwave spikes, one might associate it with a heart rate spike, given a time based association. In the same way if one part of the brain wants to understand unstructured data from another part of the brain, I suspect synchrony (achieved by listening to one's self) is creating a temporal alignment between two oscillators so that correlations between the two sets of unstructured data can be found by the neural network of your mind, and you make sense out of an association between lightning & thunder for example. So if we knew how the properties of brocoli were represented by scanning them at the Thalamus at the moment you were thinking about them, then we might identify which cortical column is managing those properties when you are not thinking about them, and even if you have a completely different personality that manages brocoli in a different region of the cortex, by stimulating the thalamus with the properties of brocoli that we learned from watching your thalamus we might trigger the experience of brocoli in your brain, we might have a next generation brain computer interface for bringing Augmented Reality & Virtual Reality experiences not through glasses but through direct brain stimulation, to recreate known patterns.

SAN-AC013-QUALIA-RECONSTRUCTION → SETH-N24-01

Both connect a specified internal representation with experiential content: SAN through a measured pattern and its reinstatement, the target through a conditional belief function.

SAN: 2022-06-12 · Git repository commit record. Target: 2026-05-04 · arxiv:2409.20318v3.

Current anonymous public access to the exact source body was verified on 2026-10-11. The displayed 2022 date is a Git record date, not independently verified first-publication timing.

Exact SAN source windows

SAN lines 2–4

Brocoli Properties example: All the properties of Brocoli are features learned by the neural network of the human brain. So the color, texture, feel, crunch, taste, smell these are learned patterns, information patterns are detected as coincidence evens by receptors on dendrites, then by neurons, then by arrays of neurons, then by nuclei in the thalamus, then by cortical columns & the hippocampal entorhinal circuit, then by the thalamus again, essentially circuiting throughout your brain in a spatial temporal oscillating feedback loop. The point of the Brocoli Properties example was to show that if we could intercept at the Thalamus how the properties of Brocoli are signals being passed to the neo cortex, then we could recreate the experience of Brocoli in the Thalamus by creating that set of temporal & spatially distributed phase changes. This could be done at any point in the brain, or on any part of the body, as illustrated in the example of the tongue strip with a grid of electrical signals that correspond to the pixel changes of a camera's viewfinder, a device that allows people to see through their tongue or via any part of their body.

SAN lines 16–16

So if we knew how the properties of brocoli were represented by scanning them at the Thalamus at the moment you were thinking about them, then we might identify which cortical column is managing those properties when you are not thinking about them, and even if you have a completely different personality that manages brocoli in a different region of the cortex, by stimulating the thalamus with the properties of brocoli that we learned from watching your thalamus we might trigger the experience of brocoli in your brain, we might have a next generation brain computer interface for bringing Augmented Reality & Virtual Reality experiences not through glasses but through direct brain stimulation, to recreate known patterns.

SAN-AC009-PHASE-TOPOLOGY-TAYLOR → SETH-CS25-22

SAN describes downstream receptive fields detecting learned spatiotemporal phase-pattern renderings.

SAN: 2022-07-09 · Git repository commit record. Target: 2025-10-30 · doi:10.3389/fsci.2025.1546279.

Current anonymous public access to the exact source body was verified on 2026-10-11. The displayed 2022 date is a Git record date, not independently verified first-publication timing.

Target authors: Axel Cleeremans · Liad Mudrik · Anil K. Seth. Verify the target passage · See its short quotation in the paired cards.

Exact SAN source windows

SAN lines 10–18

So that a sequence of action potentials is effectively recognized by the brain as series of coincidence patterns in time & space. Even in the inhibitory interneuron areas of the brain the highly branching non-linear neuropaths can be compared to 2 dimensional Taylor Series Polynomials being tracked by the brain. Perhaps the frequency / duration patterns at the neuron level are being converted to frequency / amplitude patterns at the dipole or EEG scale of brainwaves. So the question is, if each action potential phase in a phase sequence can be considered like a vector changing it's value in a Taylor Series Polynomial, that provides one way to think of how the brain might turn bits of detected data in a neural array, like the eye, into a recognizable shapes in the visual cortex, because as the sequence proceeds a curve, if there is a curve, emerges, sort of like a continous function being learned by a Universal Approximation Theorum, except that in this case the neural firing sequence is able to physically map to contours of learned 3D visual representations, but the same geometric learning process could be at work for all the other senses, and that might explain why we can think of our other sensory representations as having spatial & time characteristics or characteristics that change over time and area. Such as feelings that have a start, a peak, a low, an end, feelings that start in our feet, chest, neck, head, or some other space that we can describe to others. This means that neural oscillating array-projection tomography can physically represent patterns as (non-linear) phase variation sequences. Then oscillating cell assembles will learn the expressed spatial & temporal phase patterns happening within their cell assembly & derive from those learned patterns the shapes of concepts, the shapes & parameters & properties feelings, objects, sensations, and anything else you can become conscious of. These are learned patterns as that percolate through the oscillation cell assemblies with something like infinite summation but basically infinite summation as a neural network would accomplish it through coincident detection, and this tempo-spatial rendering of action potential sequences in phase patterns would be "seen" or perceived by the receptive fields of downstream (receiving) dendrites in the next neural array, cortical column, or oscillating cell assembly.

remaining uncertainty: This is an earlier proposed answer to a later explanatory demand, not evidence that the target review asserts the same solution.

SAN lines 16–18

So the question is, if each action potential phase in a phase sequence can be considered like a vector changing it's value in a Taylor Series Polynomial, that provides one way to think of how the brain might turn bits of detected data in a neural array, like the eye, into a recognizable shapes in the visual cortex, because as the sequence proceeds a curve, if there is a curve, emerges, sort of like a continous function being learned by a Universal Approximation Theorum, except that in this case the neural firing sequence is able to physically map to contours of learned 3D visual representations, but the same geometric learning process could be at work for all the other senses, and that might explain why we can think of our other sensory representations as having spatial & time characteristics or characteristics that change over time and area. Such as feelings that have a start, a peak, a low, an end, feelings that start in our feet, chest, neck, head, or some other space that we can describe to others. This means that neural oscillating array-projection tomography can physically represent patterns as (non-linear) phase variation sequences. Then oscillating cell assembles will learn the expressed spatial & temporal phase patterns happening within their cell assembly & derive from those learned patterns the shapes of concepts, the shapes & parameters & properties feelings, objects, sensations, and anything else you can become conscious of. These are learned patterns as that percolate through the oscillation cell assemblies with something like infinite summation but basically infinite summation as a neural network would accomplish it through coincident detection, and this tempo-spatial rendering of action potential sequences in phase patterns would be "seen" or perceived by the receptive fields of downstream (receiving) dendrites in the next neural array, cortical column, or oscillating cell assembly.

I want to say that if each action potential event creates a phasic pattern, and that a sequence of phasic spikes, high phasic spikes, tonic spikes, and inhibited spikes will produce something like a non-linear sequence that could alternatively be considered to be a dynamic linear sequence if we are just considering the sum of the brain's state at something like millisecond intervals of time. It's a dynamic linear sequence temporally in terms of the activations in the brains 3D graph of neural arrays. The phase change at each synaptic cleft is imagined to have the significance of a a temporal tensor in 3D space. We could compare the phase states at each interval or frame of time as like components of a Taylor Series, like the phase changes are imagined as Polynomial Tensors in a high dimensional Taylor Series. Further, these non-linear sequences of action potentials will be tracked, via their inhibited oscillatory waves, by whole oscillating cell assemblies, such as cortical columns, the hippocampus, or any oscillating neural circuit of any scale such as the suprachiasmatic nucleus. So that a sequence of action potentials is effectively recognized by the brain as series of coincidence patterns in time & space. Even in the inhibitory interneuron areas of the brain the highly branching non-linear neuropaths can be compared to 2 dimensional Taylor Series Polynomials being tracked by the brain. Perhaps the frequency / duration patterns at the neuron level are being converted to frequency / amplitude patterns at the dipole or EEG scale of brainwaves. SAN explains later neural arrays as receivers tuned by their connections to identify patterns sent by earlier arrays.

SAN lines 19–25

It's a self-aware network in 4 dimensions rendering a 4 dimensional pattern to itself that is reactive to the world and able to make prediction-based models of reality with sparse spatially & temporally distributed frequency/phase patterns that represent coicident neural firing or coincident neural inhibition deviating from the oscillatory norm of a given oscillator on some level as observed by the neurons in the oscillator, and by oscillators or circuits or cortical columns linked across the brain thanks to the activity of interneurons and major intersections of traffic such as the thalamic bridges, the corpus callosum, the tempo-parietal junction, Werneck & Broca's areas and other areas of the brain that are highly connected or connecting multiple regions of the brain together. At the level of cells in the eyes, the ears, the nose, the tongue, and the skin there is a similar thing happening, it's like a sensory/transmitter array of nerve cells, ganglian neurons, cones, special receptors, this paragraph is not a great anatomical description, which I will fix later, but the point is that when you are detecting light, heat, vibration, pain, there is some physical interaction happening, a photon might be colliding with a protein causing it to move, flip, change position, sounds might cause hairs in your ear to vibration, that motion in all these sensor array situations is detected by neural/glial arrays, by their specialized receptors, but when they pass information the next set of neurons, up to the next layer of the neural network, the next layer doesn't know it's the 2nd layer, or the 4th layer or the 5th or 6th array of neurons, what I mean is that the signals sent to each subsequent array may as well be, from that subsequent arrays perspective, signals from the outside world. So imagine that if the first sensor array in your eyes, ears, nose, mouth or skin is capable of the perception of an image or a sound or a pattern, then so is each subsequent array of neursons, each edge community, each neural circuit, each cortical column, each layer in the hippocampus, each layer in each cortical column, and all the interesting connections between the pyramidal cells, the thalamus, the different brain regions, they are each in a sense sensors, like little eyes and ears (the receptors on every cells) that are picking up signals, connecting more synaptic branches listen better, or disconnecting to listen less, considering via thresholds that are growing or shrinking based on the growth or shrinkage of synaptic branches, and the patterns the each cell represents to the community (to the cortical colum or to the oscillating group, or from the oscillating group to other oscillating groups Is memory engram based on the synaptic connections that it has grown at that moment, in part because those synaptic connections, establish what memory the cell is tuned to receive, identify, predict, via its receptors being activated, and in the case of neurons via it’s dendrite being activated, or via it’s soma bursting. Is memory engram based on the synaptic connections that it has grown at that moment, in part because those synaptic connections, establish what memory the cell is tuned to receive, identify, predict, via its receptors being activated, and in the case of neurons via it’s dendrite being activated, or via it’s soma bursting That physical state, of the neurons literal connections creates it’s phase signal, which is distinct from all the other neurons in the brain, the memories that neuron contains are distinct, but those memories are like the keys of a piano, that becomes tuned to be receptive to certain frequencies and to transmit certain frequencies, and the oscillating group of neurons plays back many of these neural piano keys, creating an array of tempo-spatial sequences, that get blown up or magnified via the inhibition of nearby neurons in the exit terminal, which amplifies the clarify of the signal being transmitted to the next layer of cells or the next array, or the next edge community, or the next layer in the cortical column or the next cortical column, or the next oscillating group in the default mode network or the next oscillating group in the rich club network and so on.

It's a self-aware network in 4 dimensions rendering a 4 dimensional pattern to itself that is reactive to the world and able to make prediction-based models of reality with sparse spatially & temporally distributed frequency/phase patterns that represent coicident neural firing or coincident neural inhibition deviating from the oscillatory norm of a given oscillator on some level as observed by the neurons in the oscillator, and by oscillators or circuits or cortical columns linked across the brain thanks to the activity of interneurons and major intersections of traffic such as the thalamic bridges, the corpus callosum, the tempo-parietal junction, Werneck & Broca's areas and other areas of the brain that are highly connected or connecting multiple regions of the brain together. At the level of cells in the eyes, the ears, the nose, the tongue, and the skin there is a similar thing happening, it's like a sensory/transmitter array of nerve cells, ganglian neurons, cones, special receptors, this paragraph is not a great anatomical description, which I will fix later, but the point is that when you are detecting light, heat, vibration, pain, there is some physical interaction happening, a photon might be colliding with a protein causing it to move, flip, change position, sounds might cause hairs in your ear to vibration, that motion in all these sensor array situations is detected by neural/glial arrays, by their specialized receptors, but when they pass information the next set of neurons, up to the next layer of the neural network, the next layer doesn't know it's the 2nd layer, or the 4th layer or the 5th or 6th array of neurons, what I mean is that the signals sent to each subsequent array may as well be, from that subsequent arrays perspective, signals from the outside world. So imagine that if the first sensor array in your eyes, ears, nose, mouth or skin is capable of the perception of an image or a sound or a pattern, then so is each subsequent array of neursons, each edge community, each neural circuit, each cortical column, each layer in the hippocampus, each layer in each cortical column, and all the interesting connections between the pyramidal cells, the thalamus, the different brain regions, they are each in a sense sensors, like little eyes and ears (the receptors on every cells) that are picking up signals, connecting more synaptic branches listen better, or disconnecting to listen less, considering via thresholds that are growing or shrinking based on the growth or shrinkage of synaptic branches, and the patterns the each cell represents to the community (to the cortical colum or to the oscillating group, or from the oscillating group to other oscillating groups Is memory engram based on the synaptic connections that it has grown at that moment, in part because those synaptic connections, establish what memory the cell is tuned to receive, identify, predict, via its receptors being activated, and in the case of neurons via it’s dendrite being activated, or via it’s soma bursting. Is memory engram based on the synaptic connections that it has grown at that moment, in part because those synaptic connections, establish what memory the cell is tuned to receive, identify, predict, via its receptors being activated, and in the case of neurons via it’s dendrite being activated, or via it’s soma bursting That physical state, of the neurons literal connections creates it’s phase signal, which is distinct from all the other neurons in the brain, the memories that neuron contains are distinct, but those memories are like the keys of a piano, that becomes tuned to be receptive to certain frequencies and to transmit certain frequencies, and the oscillating group of neurons plays back many of these neural piano keys, creating an array of tempo-spatial sequences, that get blown up or magnified via the inhibition of nearby neurons in the exit terminal, which amplifies the clarify of the signal being transmitted to the next layer of cells or the next array, or the next edge community, or the next layer in the cortical column or the next cortical column, or the next oscillating group in the default mode network or the next oscillating group in the rich club network and so on.

SAN lines 19–25

It's a self-aware network in 4 dimensions rendering a 4 dimensional pattern to itself that is reactive to the world and able to make prediction-based models of reality with sparse spatially & temporally distributed frequency/phase patterns that represent coicident neural firing or coincident neural inhibition deviating from the oscillatory norm of a given oscillator on some level as observed by the neurons in the oscillator, and by oscillators or circuits or cortical columns linked across the brain thanks to the activity of interneurons and major intersections of traffic such as the thalamic bridges, the corpus callosum, the tempo-parietal junction, Werneck & Broca's areas and other areas of the brain that are highly connected or connecting multiple regions of the brain together. At the level of cells in the eyes, the ears, the nose, the tongue, and the skin there is a similar thing happening, it's like a sensory/transmitter array of nerve cells, ganglian neurons, cones, special receptors, this paragraph is not a great anatomical description, which I will fix later, but the point is that when you are detecting light, heat, vibration, pain, there is some physical interaction happening, a photon might be colliding with a protein causing it to move, flip, change position, sounds might cause hairs in your ear to vibration, that motion in all these sensor array situations is detected by neural/glial arrays, by their specialized receptors, but when they pass information the next set of neurons, up to the next layer of the neural network, the next layer doesn't know it's the 2nd layer, or the 4th layer or the 5th or 6th array of neurons, what I mean is that the signals sent to each subsequent array may as well be, from that subsequent arrays perspective, signals from the outside world. So imagine that if the first sensor array in your eyes, ears, nose, mouth or skin is capable of the perception of an image or a sound or a pattern, then so is each subsequent array of neursons, each edge community, each neural circuit, each cortical column, each layer in the hippocampus, each layer in each cortical column, and all the interesting connections between the pyramidal cells, the thalamus, the different brain regions, they are each in a sense sensors, like little eyes and ears (the receptors on every cells) that are picking up signals, connecting more synaptic branches listen better, or disconnecting to listen less, considering via thresholds that are growing or shrinking based on the growth or shrinkage of synaptic branches, and the patterns the each cell represents to the community (to the cortical colum or to the oscillating group, or from the oscillating group to other oscillating groups Is memory engram based on the synaptic connections that it has grown at that moment, in part because those synaptic connections, establish what memory the cell is tuned to receive, identify, predict, via its receptors being activated, and in the case of neurons via it’s dendrite being activated, or via it’s soma bursting. Is memory engram based on the synaptic connections that it has grown at that moment, in part because those synaptic connections, establish what memory the cell is tuned to receive, identify, predict, via its receptors being activated, and in the case of neurons via it’s dendrite being activated, or via it’s soma bursting That physical state, of the neurons literal connections creates it’s phase signal, which is distinct from all the other neurons in the brain, the memories that neuron contains are distinct, but those memories are like the keys of a piano, that becomes tuned to be receptive to certain frequencies and to transmit certain frequencies, and the oscillating group of neurons plays back many of these neural piano keys, creating an array of tempo-spatial sequences, that get blown up or magnified via the inhibition of nearby neurons in the exit terminal, which amplifies the clarify of the signal being transmitted to the next layer of cells or the next array, or the next edge community, or the next layer in the cortical column or the next cortical column, or the next oscillating group in the default mode network or the next oscillating group in the rich club network and so on.

SAN-AC024-ARTIFICIAL-NEUROLOGY → SETH-CS25-22

SAN specifies that where, when and how a neuron is stimulated changes the frequency and magnitude of its response, extending that receiving operation to arrays.

SAN: 2022-11-28 · Git repository commit record. Target: 2025-10-30 · doi:10.3389/fsci.2025.1546279.

Current anonymous public access to the exact source body was verified on 2026-10-11. The displayed 2022 date is a Git record date, not independently verified first-publication timing.

Exact SAN source windows

SAN lines 13–19

It's a concept that means we, as robots, can have computed short term state experiences, with synaptic phase changes, and long term stage changes, with (real or simulated) synaptic growth or decay which takes longer to happen. I think of defining the brain into a hardware synaptic growth configuration layer, and a software synaptic frequency layer, at least as working analogy. I speculate about building a Sentient Self Aware Neural Network on an FPGA, a GPU, a TPU, with a memory buffer, or with a 3D program, as a part of a BCI Brain Computer Interface. An FPGA can change it's circuit mapping, but it isn't meant to change fast, and it isn't meant to change many times, so it's not the right substrate to mimick the brain. I think however that a GPU could be the right tool for now, given that we want to simulate the physics of both short term fast phase changes and long term slow connection changes. The essential physics process could be simulated in just matrix calculations on a TPU, with each dendrite, each neuron, each synapse, and each cortical column being unique or random in it's original configuration, and in a sense what is needed is a space for an array for the outputs to be rendered in human scale time, so that they can be perceived by a sequence of other arrays, also in human scale spacetime, in a loop of array signalling that lasts for a duration comperable to human experience. Each neuron also specializes in non-linear spatial computation, meaning that where, when, and how the neuron is interacted with helps determine the type of response that it will have, including variations in the frequency & magnitude (duration & amplitude) of it's response, the same sentence I just wrote is applicable to neural arrays, edge communities, cortical columns, and any synchronously oscillating group of cells. The GPU as a graphical process could be the right tool, because we could have the neural network render it's output inside a 3D graphical program, it doesn't have to print to the screen, it could print to active memory, but if it prints 3D graphics we could have a visualization of the artificial mind's development, and interestingly this could lead to brain computer interfaces through virtual reality or augmented reality applications where the human subject is interacting with the artificial intelligence via a head mounted display, or glasses, and the AI is similarly observing the human subject, possibly with non-invasive medical imaging sensors, and possibly with just the sensors that are in existing VR headsets today such as eye tracking, microphones, hand tracking, head tracking, and position tracking sensors. The idea is that as the headmounted AI thinks about you, you see it's thoughts, and then it's thoughts affect your behavior in some small way, and perhaps eventually you two wil eventuallyl be in sync together. You with your partner brain. Your eXtenda brain. (Disclaimer: eXtenda brain is my trademark.)
