Exact SAN source windows

SAN lines 17–19

I speculate about building a Sentient Self Aware Neural Network on an FPGA, a GPU, a TPU, with a memory buffer, or with a 3D program, as a part of a BCI Brain Computer Interface. An FPGA can change it's circuit mapping, but it isn't meant to change fast, and it isn't meant to change many times, so it's not the right substrate to mimick the brain. I think however that a GPU could be the right tool for now, given that we want to simulate the physics of both short term fast phase changes and long term slow connection changes. The essential physics process could be simulated in just matrix calculations on a TPU, with each dendrite, each neuron, each synapse, and each cortical column being unique or random in it's original configuration, and in a sense what is needed is a space for an array for the outputs to be rendered in human scale time, so that they can be perceived by a sequence of other arrays, also in human scale spacetime, in a loop of array signalling that lasts for a duration comperable to human experience. Each neuron also specializes in non-linear spatial computation, meaning that where, when, and how the neuron is interacted with helps determine the type of response that it will have, including variations in the frequency & magnitude (duration & amplitude) of it's response, the same sentence I just wrote is applicable to neural arrays, edge communities, cortical columns, and any synchronously oscillating group of cells. The GPU as a graphical process could be the right tool, because we could have the neural network render it's output inside a 3D graphical program, it doesn't have to print to the screen, it could print to active memory, but if it prints 3D graphics we could have a visualization of the artificial mind's development, and interestingly this could lead to brain computer interfaces through virtual reality or augmented reality applications where the human subject is interacting with the artificial intelligence via a head mounted display, or glasses, and the AI is similarly observing the human subject, possibly with non-invasive medical imaging sensors, and possibly with just the sensors that are in existing VR headsets today such as eye tracking, microphones, hand tracking, head tracking, and position tracking sensors. The idea is that as the headmounted AI thinks about you, you see it's thoughts, and then it's thoughts affect your behavior in some small way, and perhaps eventually you two wil eventuallyl be in sync together. You with your partner brain. Your eXtenda brain. (Disclaimer: eXtenda brain is my trademark.)

SAN lines 13–19

It's a concept that means we, as robots, can have computed short term state experiences, with synaptic phase changes, and long term stage changes, with (real or simulated) synaptic growth or decay which takes longer to happen. I think of defining the brain into a hardware synaptic growth configuration layer, and a software synaptic frequency layer, at least as working analogy. I speculate about building a Sentient Self Aware Neural Network on an FPGA, a GPU, a TPU, with a memory buffer, or with a 3D program, as a part of a BCI Brain Computer Interface. Imagine that Synaptic Frequency is a neural correlate, or the information configuration of a cell in terms of it's embodied frequencies is a neural correlate for state, or the state of your experience of reality at a given moment. Exact SAN source windows

SAN lines 3–3

To the reader: Think of yourself as an organic robot to sort of anchor your focus abstractly in the discussion of this book. Of course by some implied definition a robot is something man made, and by implied definition a human being is not manmade, but shaped by evolution, a product of nature and nurture. Yet this is useful in the same way that Dennet's conjecture that we are not seeing redness, we are just experiencing the illusion that we are seeing redness. Imagining that you are an organic robot is useful to thinking about cognition as a computational process. Neurons and other cells are doing complex non-linear spatial & temporal computations, so it's not a straight forward computation like what your computer in 2022 is doing, but even so there is a computational process that can be understood, and the comparison of consciousness to computational rendering is valid, useful, and correct.

SETH-SELECTED-40-39 · Additional partial-match candidate; independent review pending

SAN-AC024-ARTIFICIAL-NEUROLOGY → SETH-N24-05

SAN connects computational neural-state organization with experiential states and rendered output, providing a qualitative precursor to a representational/experiential relation.

SAN: 2022-11-28 · Git repository commit record. Target: 2026-05-04 · arxiv:2409.20318v3.

Current anonymous public access to the exact source body was verified on 2026-10-11. The displayed 2022 date is a Git record date, not independently verified first-publication timing.

Target authors: Lancelot Da Costa · Anil K. Seth · Karl Friston · Maxwell J. D. Ramstead · Lars Sandved-Smith. Verify the target passage · See its short quotation in the paired cards.

Exact SAN source windows

SAN lines 7–19

Imagine that Synaptic Frequency is a neural correlate, or the information configuration of a cell in terms of it's embodied frequencies is a neural correlate for state, or the state of your experience of reality at a given moment. Imagine that Synaptic Connections via Growth LTP & Decay LTD, over a long term, results in eventual threshold changes that we can identify as Stages, or major changes in live perspective that only happen after a very long time, or only happen after a very big life event. The concept of Cognitive States & Stages comes from either AQAL (All Quadrants All Lines) or Spiral Dynamics, I forget which, and it doesn't matter. It's a concept that means we, as robots, can have computed short term state experiences, with synaptic phase changes, and long term stage changes, with (real or simulated) synaptic growth or decay which takes longer to happen. I think of defining the brain into a hardware synaptic growth configuration layer, and a software synaptic frequency layer, at least as working analogy. I speculate about building a Sentient Self Aware Neural Network on an FPGA, a GPU, a TPU, with a memory buffer, or with a 3D program, as a part of a BCI Brain Computer Interface. An FPGA can change it's circuit mapping, but it isn't meant to change fast, and it isn't meant to change many times, so it's not the right substrate to mimick the brain. I think however that a GPU could be the right tool for now, given that we want to simulate the physics of both short term fast phase changes and long term slow connection changes. The essential physics process could be simulated in just matrix calculations on a TPU, with each dendrite, each neuron, each synapse, and each cortical column being unique or random in it's original configuration, and in a sense what is needed is a space for an array for the outputs to be rendered in human scale time, so that they can be perceived by a sequence of other arrays, also in human scale spacetime, in a loop of array signalling that lasts for a duration comperable to human experience. Each neuron also specializes in non-linear spatial computation, meaning that where, when, and how the neuron is interacted with helps determine the type of response that it will have, including variations in the frequency & magnitude (duration & amplitude) of it's response, the same sentence I just wrote is applicable to neural arrays, edge communities, cortical columns, and any synchronously oscillating group of cells. The GPU as a graphical process could be the right tool, because we could have the neural network render it's output inside a 3D graphical program, it doesn't have to print to the screen, it could print to active memory, but if it prints 3D graphics we could have a visualization of the artificial mind's development, and interestingly this could lead to brain computer interfaces through virtual reality or augmented reality applications where the human subject is interacting with the artificial intelligence via a head mounted display, or glasses, and the AI is similarly observing the human subject, possibly with non-invasive medical imaging sensors, and possibly with just the sensors that are in existing VR headsets today such as eye tracking, microphones, hand tracking, head tracking, and position tracking sensors. The idea is that as the headmounted AI thinks about you, you see it's thoughts, and then it's thoughts affect your behavior in some small way, and perhaps eventually you two wil eventuallyl be in sync together. You with your partner brain. Your eXtenda brain. (Disclaimer: eXtenda brain is my trademark.)

reason: SAN connects computational neural-state organization with experiential states and rendered output, providing a qualitative precursor to a representational/experiential relation.

target chain: Belief/phenomenology correspondence -> structured experiential domain -> formal comparisons and predictions.

reason: SAN connects computational neural-state organization with experiential states and rendered output, providing a qualitative precursor to a representational/experiential relation.

residual: Three-dimensional graphics and a high-dimensional probabilistic geometry are not automatically the same space. The overlap is limited to the state/content bridge.

L1

result: PARTIAL_OPERATION

reason: Both descriptions use recurrent neural dynamics over time with downstream outputs. SAN additionally proposes changing connections and experience; the target fixes reservoir weights and trains a linear readout.

L2

result: PARTIAL_CAUSAL_ARCHITECTURE

reason: The shared input/state/output dependency is real and survives implementation differences. A loop alone does not establish reservoir training, a task loss, or the target's bidirectional performance/emergence result.

L3

result: SUPPORTED_CONSTRAINED_OUTPUT_RECONSTRUCTION_WITH_NONUNIQUE_SOLUTION

reason: Constrained result: Forecast temporal trajectories from the observed input history; the selected paper realizes this goal with a reservoir and learned readout. Required operation: Encode task-relevant input/history in usable state distinctions and map those distinctions to predictions. Source test: SAN line 19 provides time-extended signaling loops, nonlinear spatial-temporal responses and subsequent arrays. It does not specify a fixed reservoir or readout-only optimization. Alternatives: Reservoir recurrence plus trained readout; Other recurrent state models; History windows or delay-line features in nonrecurrent predictors. Remaining uncertainty: SAN's loop is a plausible architectural ingredient, not a derivation of the target equations, optimization or PID emergence measure.

Limits and next evidence

The target cites Jaeger (2001), Maass et al. (2002), Buonomano/Maass (2009) for the architecture and Rosas/Mediano et al. (2020) for emergence. These citations are prior-work boundaries, not a new author-wide award.

Receiver use: SAN formulation alongside the earlier workspace account

SAN offers a specific receiver-use proposal: learned neural structure recognizes an input and shapes what is transmitted next. Dehaene and Naccache (2001) already describe selective processors and sustained workspace access; the inspected passages do not give the same selected cellular chain. The 2025 review asks the receiver question rather than endorsing SAN’s answer.

Predictive rendering: keep SAN’s implementation and the older predictive account distinct

SAN’s 2022 record describes predictive rendering through spatially and temporally distributed phase/frequency patterns, including excitation and inhibition. The selected 2025 review shares the broader internal-construction theme. Seth’s 2013 paper already describes top-down predictions constrained by sensory errors, so 2025 is a later document comparison—not the origin date of that predictive account. Earlier SAN formulations remain part of the open genealogy.

Recurrent computation: preserve the SAN assembly without claiming reservoir firstness

SAN’s artificial-neurology proposal combines fast phase changes, slower connection changes and recurrent rendering. It shares a processing sequence with the selected 2024 reservoir-computing paper. Jaeger’s earlier report already supplies recurrent states and trained readouts; the recovered PDF is the 2010 correction of the 2001 report. The retained partial comparison does not claim SAN invented reservoir computing.

SAN specifies a personal-pattern reconstruction route

SAN’s 2022 record proposes measuring a person’s sensory pattern and recreating experience with corresponding stimulation. Metzinger’s 2009 book already proposes technical control of experiential content; the inspected pages do not specify SAN’s personal-pattern measurement-and-replay chain. The selected 2025 review shares the intervention goal, explicitly names phenotechnology, and leaves the neural code unspecified.

SAN connects learned content to evolving phase patterns and downstream reception

SAN describes learned content as evolving phase patterns that downstream neural arrays receive. Chang and Tsao (2017) show a face-coordinate code; Malach (2021) connects experiential content with synaptic relational geometry and recurrent binding. Those overlaps remain visible. The inspected sources do not specify SAN’s phase-sequence reception chain, and SAN’s qualitative proposal is not the same as their measured geometry. Earlier SAN formulations still need comparison with those sources.

SAN supplies a physical rendering proposal alongside formal experience models

SAN’s June/July 2022 records propose a physical phase-pattern account of rendering and receiving content. The March 2022 generative-passages paper already links formal experience models with constraints on neural explanations, while leaving implementation open. The later Rosetta version adds an explicit belief-to-phenomenology assumption. The partial state/content connection remains; neither a shared equation nor complete equivalence has been demonstrated.
